[2026-10-13 19:49] kotryk : ​Fair point. On top of that, today I have a concern regarding funding based on the league tier. When promoting to a higher league, is the promotion reward—and more importantly, the sponsor level—proportionately higher? Because if I were to be promoted today to a higher league where I know I'll be playing youth players and we'll lose almost every game, wouldn't it be better to intentionally stay in my current league, build up popularity, if the sponsor money is the same anyway?
[2026-10-14 05:54] grillanza_60069 : I think sponsor should be connected to DH. Starting from next season differences will be higher and higher
[2026-10-14 06:37] isr_shay : I think the general sponsorship income should be the same for everyone, for several reasons, but mainly to avoid widening the gap between new and established players.  New players need a fair chance to build and establish their clubs  However, I do think clubs should earn more money from league matches as they progress to higher divisions so more fans come  Additionally, I think there should be an option to expand the capacity of their home arena, allowing clubs to increase their matchday revenue over time.   I’d just like to add that, in my opinion, economic growth and expanding arena seating capacity should be a slow and gradual process, at a pace determined by the developer. Growing too quickly or generating too much income isn't always a good thing.

[2026-10-22 15:40] isr_shay : Yes, but I think he has a valid point. In my opinion, there should be some restrictions. I would suggest   1. limiting transfers to 10 per season (the exact number is up to the development team and can be adjusted). However, it's important to remember that both buying and selling a player should count as a transfer. If some teams are constantly flipping players and making 50 transfers per season, it can make it harder for other teams to progress and may also discourage new players from joining or staying in the game. I think some reasonable limits would help keep the game fair, balanced, and welcoming to new managers.  2. Instead of limiting the number of transfers per season, we could introduce a progressive tax based on how many players a team sells during a season.    For example:    - 1–10 sales: 5% tax (current rate)    - 11–20 sales: 12% tax    - 21–30 sales: 20% tax    - 31+ sales: 35% tax     This way, regular managers won't be affected, while teams making 50–60 transfers per season for profit will face higher costs.    It won't prevent trading, but it will make excessive player flipping less profitable while keeping the transfer market open to everyone.    The numbers are just examples and can be adjusted by the development team.
[2026-10-22 15:52] grobarbrate : Honestly i cant agree with that. Cuz in every game we had players that buys and sells a lot of players. So put a cap on buying and selling is not good idea , anyone can play the game how he wants. 2. Little better idea but again , you cant take 35% of money of someone just bcs he made 30 transfers. Meybe idea can be to make it way harder to become ultra rich and just to save the money and doing nothing. So , for example , you can collect 10m without any penalty. If you have more than 10 you are paying 1% of your totall money like a tax per week. Thats 100k. Every week until you drop bellow 10m. If you have over 20m tax becomes 2% etc. That way we can push people to spend money not only to earn and save...
[2026-10-22 15:55] dollarius_80489 : I do not agree with restrictions on the market. My focus is on the team performance, I'd prefer an in-game penalty, as a lack of team chemistry

[2026-10-22 15:58] isr_shay : I understand your point, and I actually agree that my suggestion sounds quite aggressive. But that's mainly because I intentionally used extreme numbers to illustrate the idea. of course, the actual limits, percentages, and penalties would need to be reviewed and balanced by the developers. The numbers I mentioned were just examples, not something I think should be implemented exactly as suggested. I also agree that everyone should be free to play the game their own way, including buying and selling as many players as they want. my main concern is simply the long-term balance of the game's economy. I think we both want the same thing, just have different ideas about how to achieve it.  Your tax idea is interesting too, although I think we should be careful not to punish managers who save money for several seasons as part of a long-term strategy.

[2026-10-17 10:29] shakezito : Se o projeto das seleções avançar e, mais tarde, surgirem também ligas nacionais, penso que será fundamental mantermos este espírito de união, evitando rivalidades desnecessárias. O objetivo deve ser partilharmos conhecimentos, aprendermos uns com os outros e evoluirmos em conjunto, procurando fazer de Portugal uma das grandes potências dentro do jogo.
[2026-10-17 10:30] shakezito : Na minha opinião, a prioridade de cada treinador deve ser sempre a sua própria equipa e os seus jogadores. As seleções devem ser encaradas como uma oportunidade extra de contribuir para a comunidade, e não como uma prioridade acima dos objetivos individuais de cada um. Naturalmente, isso poderá ser diferente para quem não tem como principal objetivo alcançar resultados a curto prazo. Tenho gostado de ver a forma como todos vão partilhando ideias e experiências sobre treino, potencial dos jogadores e outros aspetos do jogo. Acredito que este tipo de partilha é essencial para crescermos enquanto comunidade e para que todos possamos beneficiar do conhecimento coletivo. Queria também deixar uma primeira sugestão: investirmos mais no desenvolvimento dos nossos próprios jogadores, começando, naturalmente, pela academia. Sobretudo para quem está agora a começar, acho importante perceber que a academia pode ser a base para construir um projeto sólido e sustentável a longo prazo, em vez de canalizar grande parte dos recursos para o mercado, na contratação de jogadores estrangeiros já mais velhos. Se conseguirmos apostar na formação, partilhar conhecimento e manter uma visão comum para o futuro, acredito que podemos criar bases muito fortes, tanto para cada uma das nossas equipas como para o crescimento de Portugal dentro do jogo.
